| Multiplying by thousands — Bhajan Buys Bricks: old bricks Rs 1200 per thousand, Intapur Rs 1800, Brickabad Rs 2000; 3000 Brickabad bricks = Rs 6000; 500 old bricks = Rs 600 | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 12 |
| Buses needed — each bus has 50 seats: 4 buses take 50x4 = 200, not enough for 210 — a 5th bus is needed | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 23 |
| Distributing lunch to 210 children — each child gets 1 orange, 1 banana, 5 biscuits: 210 oranges, 1050 biscuits; 38 children take no banana, so 172 bananas | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 30 |

| Mental multiplication strategies — in a week one rickshaw earns 20x6 = Rs 120 (no Sunday rent); do mentally: 2x6, 4x80, 20x6, 4x81 (4 more than 4x80), 2x60, 9x25, 3x42, 31x9; 31 kg newspaper at Rs 5 = Rs 155 | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 63 |
| Two-digit x two-digit, box method — Dinu pays for 63 kg plastic at Rs 12: 12x63 in boxes 600+120+30+6 = Rs 756; Kiran's profit Rs 2 a kg, Rs 126 on 63 kg | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 65 |
| Three-digit x one-digit, split method — 32 kg iron at Rs 14 and Kiran's Rs 2-a-kg profit (Rs 64); 152 kg newspaper at Rs 6: 600+300+12 = Rs 912, profit Rs 152; 4 kg brass at Rs 180 | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 66 |
| Arrays and factor pairs — Tables and Shares: Shyama's flower-beds — 18 = 6x3 (6 rows of 3), 18 in one row, 18 in other row splits; draw a 48-plant garden in equal rows | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 120 |
| Arrays for a total — Bheema's shelf for 30 jars in two equal rows (15 each); other ways to arrange 30 jars in equal rows | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 121 |

| Packing problems — Rabiya: 4 laddoos a box, 23 boxes need 92, the tray has 80 — not enough, 12 more needed; Shally: 338 soap bars, 85 a carton (4 cartons); Manpreet: 1500 sacks, 250 a truck = 6 trips at Rs 500 = Rs 3000 | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 126 |
| Making your own questions (image pages) — write the question for: 8 packets of 6 rakhis; 10 packets of sugar for Rs 110 in all; 35 students in 7 equal rows | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 131 |
| Making your own questions (image pages) — write the question for: 4 people, one rail ticket Rs 62; cloth at Rs 20 a metre, Rs 140 paid (= 7 metres) | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 132 |
| Cost tables — Factory set-up cost table: unit price × number of items, total cost | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 14 |
| Distributive chunking — Bela's method: multiply by splitting (65 × 31 as 60 × 30 + 5 × 30 + 60 × 1 + 5 × 1; 195 × 31 with a zero row) | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 170 |
| Applications — Salaries for a month and a year; glasses of water, heartbeats (72 × 60 × 24…) and milk over days/years | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 172 |
| Multiplication patterns — Patterns: (12 × 9) + 3 = 111, (123 × 9) + 4 = 1111…; aaa × aaa; the age trick × 7 × 13 × 11; the 142857 cycle | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 178 |
| Word problems — Multi-step multiplication/division problems (screws per month, radio sets, distance × speed) | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 17 |
| Number line — Multiplication as repeated jumps on the number line (3 × 4 = 12) | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 31 |
| Multiplying integers — Positive × negative by repeated addition on the number line (3 × (−5) = −15) | India, Mathematics Class 7, printed p. 10 |
| Sign rules — Negative × positive by pattern (−3 × 5 = −15); product of two negatives is positive, by descending pattern | India, Mathematics Class 7, printed p. 11 |
| Sign rules — Sign of a product of three or more negatives: even count of negatives → positive, odd count → negative; (−1) multiplied even/odd times | India, Mathematics Class 7, printed p. 14 |
| Adding three numbers — adding in sequence (Дараалаан нэмэх хасах): 4 + 2 + 3 on ten-frames, 2 + 1 + 1 and 1 + 3 + 4 on number lines | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 64 |
| Adding three numbers — 1 + 2 + 4 with counters and ten-frames (1 + 2 = 3, 3 + 4); join numbered dots in a counting puzzle | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 65 |
| Grouping to make ten first — easy ways to add three numbers: 5 + 2 + 8 against 8 + 2 + 5 and 8 + 5 + 2 — "it is easier to start with the pair that makes ten"; which operation first?; apples left 8 − 2 − 3; chain sets 2+6+4, 5+6+5, 9−5+1… | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 96 |
| Counting legs, heads, ears, eyes — count for pictured rabbits and chickens: their legs, heads, ears, eyes (3 rabbits; 2 chickens; a rabbit and a chicken; 2 rabbits and 3 chickens) | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 115 |
| Counting in bundles — multiplication: counting things in bundles (Юмсыг багцлан тоолох) — three children count game pieces: Zul by ones (1, 2, 3, 4…), Bayar by fives (5, 10, 15…), Nomin by twos (2, 4, 6, 8…); Zul took longest — counting many things in bundles is quicker; count pencils bundled in twos along a 0-10 line, spoons in fives; a shop unloaded 7 boxes of biscuits packed in tens — find the total by addition | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 30 |
| Repeated addition — pencils bundled in tens — in bundles of how many, how many in all; bundle buttons in twos — how many bundles, how many buttons; bundle the pencils fully; help write the page-line divisions (+5 jumps to 50); draw 14 circles and bundle them in twos; evaluate repeated additions (2+2+2+2, 5+5+5, 10+10+10+10, 5+5+5+5+5, 10+10+10+10+10); complete the car numbers (12, 22, 32…) — two chosen cars' numbers differ by 20, which could they be? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 31 |
| The multiplication operation (Үржүүлэх үйлдэл): a seller packs 6 eggs in a box and sold 9 boxes — the boy counts in bundles 6, 12, 18…, the girl would add 6 + 6 + …, writing it out is hard; the seller records the count as "9 × 6 = 54"; discuss what each number means; hens and their eggs: how many hens, how many eggs under each, how many in all — □ × □ = □; chicks in cages with a labelled diagram (number of bundles × number in each bundle = total number of things); self-check questions on where the multiplication sign is written in 118-119 | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 32 |
| Factors and product — stars bundled in twos: □ × □ = 18 with the diagram naming first factor (1 дүгээр үржигдэхүүн), multiplication sign, second factor, product value; readings of 9 × 2: nine multiplied by two is eighteen, the product of 9 and 2 is 18, increasing 2 ninefold is 18…; write multiplications for pictured fruit (bananas, plums, strawberries, tomatoes); bundle flowers in tens and write the count as a multiplication; number-line jumps: 3 × 2 = 6 shown, and jumps of 5 to 20 as □ × □ | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 33 |
| Arrays: rows and columns — two children describe the same dot table differently (2 columns and 4 rows against 4 columns and 2 rows) — who is right?; colour each row a different colour; find each table's total cells by multiplication; a triangle array: total triangles by multiplication, triangles in the 3rd column by multiplication, in the 3rd row by multiplication; room/sum tasks on a 12-20 grid (sum of the 2nd row, of the 3rd column, what number is at 1st row 2nd column…) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 34 |
| Multiplying by two: 2 pupils sit at each desk — a table builds 1 × 2 = 2, 2 × 2 = 4, 3 × 2 = 6, 4 × 2, 5 × 2 with +1 bundle and +2 total steps; 6 × 2 to 10 × 2 found directly (the boy: the product grows by 2 each time); classroom questions (children at 5 desks, at all the pictured desks, difference between 6 desks and 8 desks); complete the two-times table in words (one two is two, two twos are four…) and colour the products on a 1-70 chart | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 37 |
| Two-times practice and games — a domino game for multiplying by two (join equal halves, write the multiplication, e.g. 2 × 3 = 6); the girl has 7 notebooks, her brother has 2 times more — how many have they in all?; a class camping in gers: first 5 children entered each of 2 gers, then 3 more children entered each ger — how many in each ger now, how many in both gers?; write multiplications for row/column bundles of squares, triangles, circles; butterflies: if the spots on a butterfly's two wings are equal, how many spots has each butterfly? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 38 |
| Two-times word problems — two children found a class's pencils in different ways — explain both; write multiplications for two bundles of pencils — are the equalities different, do equal numbers give equal values, why?; 3 glass bowls with 2 fish each and 2 bowls with 3 fish each as multiplications; number-line markings for ×2 (six jumps of 2; one long jump) written as equalities; evaluate and round to tens (9 × 2 − 11, 2 × 10 − 3, 6 × 2 + 39, 2 × 7 + 48) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 39 |
| Commutativity of multiplication — the children say the row and column counts of a multiplication table in different orders: 2 × 4 = 8 against 4 × 2 = 8 — swapping the factors' places does not change the product; write the star groups as multiplications; express 2 × 7, 2 × 8, 2 × 10, 6 × 2 in pictures; a number line with 6 jumps of 2 and a multiplication table grid (first factor down, second factor across; the cells for 10 and 12 lit) — compare with addition; which pairs of numbers have the table's numbers as their product? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 40 |
| Products game, comparisons, two-times-more — a card game "find the product" (picture cards with products of 2, number cards 1-20); compare mentally (2 × 8 … 8 × 2; 4 × 2 … 3 × 2; 2 × 9 … 2 × 10; 7 × 2 … 2 × 7; 5 + 5 … 2 × 2 + 2 + 2 + 2 + 2; 3 + 2 × 3 … 3 + 3 + 3); a churn holds 35 litres of airag, a 2-litre vessel was used 4 times — how much is left?; the girl drew 4 planes, the boy drew 2 times more planes than the girl — "2 times more" means multiply by 2 — how many did the boy draw?; the girl has 7 notebooks, her brother 2 times more — his count; number pyramids (5 and 5 make 10…; 10 and □ make 20 and 20; □ and 15 make 30 and 60) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 41 |
| Multiplying by five: handprints pressed in sand — how many presses, how many finger marks; a table builds 1 × 5 = 5, 2 × 5 = 10, 3 × 5, 4 × 5, 5 × 5 with block columns (+1, +5 steps); bundle stones in fives and write the multiplication for the total; money in 5₮ coins: Amar, Bayarmaa, Kherlen and Tungaa's pictured coins — how many tögrög has each, who has most, Bayarmaa and Tungaa together, Amar against Kherlen by how much less; Amar puts 5₮ in his money box every day — after 7 days how much?; Khulan's house number is two-digit and one digit is 2 times the other — what could the number be? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 42 |
| Multiplying by ten: Tuya puts 10₮ in her money box every day — a table builds 1 × 10 = 10, 2 × 10, …, 5 × 10 (+1 day, +10 steps); a pictograph of club pupils where one crossed square = 10 pupils (music 10, dance 80, chess 50, art 30): how many in each club, which club is biggest / smallest, dance against art by how many more, chess and music together; complete the 0-100 line in tens; the ×10 table (× 1-10 → 10, 20, …, 50…); compare the numbers in one column of the table — what do you notice, how is it written? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 44 |
| Ten-times problems — a finger game: multiply the number of fingers shown by 10 and say the sum; how can you count the circles in a full dot table (unfinished rows/columns of tens); write expressions from words and evaluate (increase the product of 4 and 10 by 5; increase 5 by 2 then multiply by 10; decrease the product of 3 and 5 by 10); a sack of potatoes weighs more than 4 × 10 kg and less than 10 × 5 kg — how many kilograms could it be? mark it on the number line; a milkmaid milked 10 cows, each cow gave 5 litres, and 35 litres were made into yoghurt — how many litres of milk are left? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 45 |
| Multiplying by one and by zero: a ring-throwing game — a ring on a post scores 1 point, a ring that misses scores 0 points; Mendee threw 5 rings on, Selengge threw 7 rings and missed — Mendee's rings on □ × □, rings not on □ × 0; count Selengge's ring points; write multiplications for pencil and cup pictures including □ × 0; the rules in speech bubbles: multiplying a number by 0 makes 0 (3 × 0 = 0), multiplying 0 by a number makes 0 too, and swapping factors does not change the product; rock-paper-scissors: three friends showed "stone" — how many fingers in all (□ × □)? one friend showed "scissors" — how many fingers? all four friends together?; sets 1 × 7, 8 × 1, 10 × 1, 0 × 2, 0 × 3 + 17, 5 × 1 + 24, 4 × 1 − 4, 7 × 0 + 49, 6 × 0 + 31; a number line proving that multiplying by 1 gives the number itself | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 46 |
| One- and zero-times tables — complete the ×1 table (1 × 1 = 1, 3, 4 given…) and the ×0 table (0, 0, 0, …, 0 given); which expression's product is different from the others (7 × 0, 0 × 3, 8 × 0, 1 × 1, 0 × 5, 10 × 0)?; fill the boxes (□ × 5 = 5, 6 × □ = 6, 1 × □ = 7, 10 × □ = 0, 0 × □ = 0, 8 × □ = 8, 85 − □ = 85, 47 + □ = 47, □ − 100 = 0, 0 + □ = 99); compare (12 + 0 … 12 × 0; 35 − 0 … 35 × 1; 8 + 1 … 1 × 8; 27 × 1 … 35 × 0); an archery self-test: count each of four children's arrows in the 0-point and 1-point rings into a table (Khuder: 2 and 4, total 4) — who scored most, who least? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 47 |
| Arrays both ways, chains, money exchange — who is right: the girl drew 8 stars, the boy says she placed them in 2 columns and 4 rows, the girl says 2 rows and 4 columns — discuss; write the circle arrays as multiplications in two ways (а, б, в); 3 sheep have all lambed twins — how many lambs in all?; a building has 4 entrances with 10 households at each entrance — how many households, and the last door of the 3rd entrance is which household number?; number chains (1 → +6 → ? → ×10 → ?; 5 → ×2 → ? → ×10 → ?; 6 → −3 → ? → ×5 → ?); a money-template problem: grandmother gave her grandson 5₮ and 10₮ notes, the boy exchanged all his money for one 50₮ note — how many 5₮ and how many 10₮ notes could grandmother have given? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 48 |
| Multiplication self-test — self-test: write expressions from words (decrease 81 by the difference of 62 and 16; increase 29 by the sum of 47 and 15); draw a table of 2 rows and 5 columns, and of 5 rows and 2 columns; mental arithmetic crosses centred on 53 (60 − 38, 38 + 18, 27, 46, 25, 59 branches); in how many ways can the 20 pictured pencils be bundled? — write the bundlings as multiplications; a club has 31 girls, and boys 4 fewer than girls — how many boys study at the club?; write the positions of the shapes in a lettered/numbered grid in the notebook | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 50 |
| Multiplying by four, dividing by four: a game — draw a number card 1-10, count that many shapes, put counters on every corner of each shape, write the total as a multiplication (a quadrilateral with 3 counters per corner: 4 × 3 = 12); bundle boortsog (fried dough) in fours and compose multiplication and division examples; evaluate (6 × 4; 9 × 4; 7 × 4; (17 − 9) × 4; 12 ÷ 4; 28 ÷ 4; 36 ÷ 4; (24 + 16) ÷ 4); children built squares from sticks: Ösökhöö 3 squares, Tüvshin 5, Maral 4, Buyanaa 5 — how many sticks did each use, Buyanaa used 2 times more sticks than whom, name the child who used 4 fewer sticks than Maral; take 20 pencils bundled: in fours — how many pencils do you take, in fives — how many bundles; in a number table, find and colour the numbers divisible by 4 and name the column in which all the numbers are even | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 65 |
| Four-times problems and operation order preview — a café has 4 tables seating 8 each: in the morning 3 people sat at each table, at midday 5 at each, in the evening all 8 seats at every table were taken — how many people ate in the morning, at midday, in the evening, and how many were served in the whole day?; grandfather built 2 pens for his young animals: one pen is 6 m on each side, the other 8 m on each side — how many metres of fence for each pen, and the total length of both pens' fences; complete the table (dividend 16, 20, 24, □, 32; divisor □, 4, 4, 4; quotient □, 5, 6, 7); Bulgan showed 6 pencils, his younger brother 2 times more, and his younger sister 2 times fewer than the brother — how many pencils did each show in all?; complete the multiplication grids (×2 then ×2); speech bubbles: multiplying by 4 is the same as multiplying by 2 twice; doubling the two-times table gives the four-times table | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 66 |
| Multiplying by eight, dividing by eight: on a chessboard put white round shapes along the first row, red ones in the third row, blue ones in the fourth row — how many shapes of each colour were put on the board, and how many checkers in all?; Wednesday's flight information: departure and arrival clock faces, routes Ulaanbaatar–Choibalsan (7 rows with 8 people in each row), Choibalsan–Dalanzadgad (9 rows, 8 each), Dalanzadgad–Ulaanbaatar (22 fewer people than the second route) — how many people flew on each route, how many flew by plane on Wednesday in all, on the 1:00 flight in which direction did how many people fly, which route took the most time; compare the multiplication written in each column (1 × 2, 1 × 4, 1 × 8 … 10 × 2, 10 × 4, 10 × 8): how does the product change down the second multiplier's column? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 68 |
| Eight-times practice — compose multiplication and division examples for the two shagai (anklebone) pictures; compare the expressions in each column (6 × 4 + 6 × 4 over 6 × 8; 7 × 4 + 7 × 4 over 7 × 8; 8 × 4 + 8 × 4 over 8 × 8; 4 × 9 + 4 × 9 over 8 × 9 — the last combined form uses commutativity) and compare the first row's values: which times table was used?; operation chains (×4 then ×2 loops on 2, 3, 4); the boy put 4 flowers on each of 6 shelves — if he shares all the flowers equally onto 8 shelves, how many on each?; compose and solve problems (the class has 3 sections, each section has 4 desks — how many desks in the class?; 6 children shared mushrooms, each child got 8 mushrooms — how many mushrooms were there?; the number of parts is 8 and the number in each part is 7 — what is the total?); draw a picture for your word problem: 32 shapes put into □ parts (□ ÷ □ = □) and 32 shapes shared into 4 equal parts — how many in each part? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 69 |
| Eight- and four-times problems — on a 1-100 chart mark 5 numbers beginning from 16 increasing by 8 each time, and 4 numbers beginning from 36 decreasing by 4; a counter problem: there were 100 eggs on the counter, 72 eggs were put into baskets of 8 — how many baskets were used, how many eggs were left unpacked?; look at the train picture and compose a problem solved by multiplication (which member of the multiplication is the number of the train's wagons, and which is the number of its windows?); write expressions from word statements (decrease 96 by the product of 4 and 8; increase the quotient of 56 and 8 twofold; the first addend is 49 and the second addend is the product of 6 and 4; the dividend is 32 and the divisor is the quotient of 4 and …); find the missing numbers mentally (6 × 4 = 5 × 4 + □; 7 × 8 = 2 × □ + □ × 8 as printed; 8 × 9 + 9 = 8 × □; □ × 4 + 3 × 4 = 7 × 4); compose a number whose tens digit is 4 times smaller than its ones digit, and multiply it by the smallest two-digit odd number; a game: take 24 dice — bundle them 2 at a time, 4 at a time, 8 at a time — could they be shared equally each time? write the equality | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 71 |
| Multiplying by three, dividing by three: compose multiplication and division examples for the two triangle arrays (grouped by columns, grouped by rows); tree-planting day: our class planted willows in 3 rows of 8 and poplars in 4 rows of 3 — how many trees did our class plant in all?; Enrel has 24 coloured pencils and gave them to friends 3, 3 at a time — to how many friends did she give pencils?; the mushroom picture: how many mushrooms in 3 rows, in 3 columns, and the count in 1 column is how many times less than the count in 3 columns?; write the quotient that matches the number line's division (jumps of 3 up to 27) as a product check; the multiplication table with rows ×1, ×2, ×3: using the ×3 table, how can the ×3 table be obtained from the ×1 and ×2 tables (the circled 2 and 4 make 6, the circled … make 12)?; discuss which other numbers' multiplication tables can be obtained from the ×1 and ×2 tables and test it | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 72 |
| Three-times practice — children lined up in rows for PE (picture): how many rows are there, how many children in each row, how many children in all (□ × □ = □)?; Sanchir made 3 clocks and Bishrel made 3 times more clocks than Sanchir — how many clocks did Bishrel make (shown on a bar/segment diagram)?; evaluate and arrange the product and quotient values from biggest to smallest (three arrow diagrams pairing ×3 and ÷3); the children's stick shapes in a table (Khuder: 3 pentagons; Enrel: 3 squares; Zaya: 3 triangles): how many sticks did each child use, how many sticks did the children use in all, from Khuder's used sticks how many triangles can be made, can Enrel's total sticks build 4 triangles — test it; colour each pictured shape (a 2×3 rectangle, a cross-shaped grid, a 4×5 rectangle) having divided it into 3 equal parts | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 73 |
| Multiplying by six, dividing by six: the boy divided a table's cells into 3 equal parts and divided the resulting table into 2 equal parts again (□ ÷ 3 = □; □ ÷ 2 = □); the girl divided a table's cells into 6 equal parts (□ ÷ 6 = □); compare the boy's and the girl's division tables; write the numbers of the cells they divided into a table; how did they divide the table's cells, and what was the result — discuss; evaluate and compare each column's examples (2 × 3 × 2 / 2 × 3 + 2 × 3 / 2 × 6; 5 × 3 × 2 / 5 × 3 + 5 × 3 / 5 × 6; 8 × 3 × 2 / 8 × 3 + 8 × 3 / 8 × 6); compose multiplication and division examples for the two tree pictures | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 74 |
| Six-times problems — the girl's library has 54 books: she put them equally onto 6 shelves — how many books on each shelf? she put 6 books on each shelf — onto how many shelves did she put them?; compare (3 × 4 … 24 ÷ 6; 28 + 36 … 8 × 6; 5 × 7 + 7 … 6 × 7; 9 × 4 … 9 × 3 + 3; 92 − 84 … 48 ÷ 6; 5 + 6 × 5 … 6 × 6; 35 ÷ 5 × 3 … 21 × 1; 15 × 0 … 15 + 0; 36 ÷ 6 + 6 … 6 × 7); a flower-gluing problem: each child glued flowers with 6 petals; the girls glued 8 flowers and the boys glued 2 times fewer flowers than the girls — how many flowers did the boys glue, how many petals did the girls use, how many petals did both use in all for the flowers they glued?; two children describe a car park picture (3 rows with 6 cars in each row against 3 rows with 2 times more cars) — whose statement is right?; complete the division table (dividend 18, 24, □, 36, □, □; divisor 6 throughout; quotient □, 4, 5, □, □, 8): when the dividend increases by 6 how does the quotient change, and when the quotient increases by 2 which member of the operation changed — discuss; the girl's claim: if you double the ×3 table you get the ×6 table — explain her words | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 75 |
| Multiplying by nine, dividing by nine: father, son and daughter counted the cabbages grown in their vegetable field — father counted fastest (6 rows and 9: "counting by nines: nine nines are fifty-four"), the son counted 6, 12, 18, 24…, the daughter counted 1, 2, 3, 4… — how can the cabbage count be found quickly, and explain how you found it, is there an easier way?; the gardener planted flowers, 9 in each row — how many flowers did he plant in all?; the boy strung 63 mushrooms in strings of 9 to dry — how many strings of mushrooms did he dry?; Bat wrote the ×9 table and said "when you multiply by 9 the product's last digit decreases by 1 each time" — complete the table (factors …, 2, 3, …, 5, 6, …, 9, 10, 0 against 9 throughout; products □, 18, □, 36, □, 54, □, 81, □, □) and check whether Bat's statement is true; complete the number chains (7 → ×9 → □ → −18 → □ → ÷9 → □ → +85 → □ → ÷10 → □ → ×1 → □ → ? → 0; three triangles each with ×3, ×3 and ×? / ÷? below) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 76 |
| Nine-times practice — builders laid 72 bricks into 9 equal columns — how many bricks in each column?; one brick weighs 2 kilograms — how many kilograms are 9 bricks?; how many cells has each pictured table — how did you find it, discuss an easier way with others; colour each table divided into 9 equal parts; express the quotients in pictures: 18 ÷ 2 and 18 ÷ 9 — is there a difference?; complete the house tables (×9: 7 → 63, 4 → □, □ → 72, 9 → □; ÷9: 81 → 9, 63 → □, □ → 5, 27 → □); the product of 8 and 9 is near which ten — mark it on the divided line; a seller put toys on a 9-cell shelf: 3 teddy bears, 4 planes and 5 balls in each cell — how many of each toy did he put on the shelf, and how many toys in all?; complete the multiplication table and discuss how the ×9 table connects with the ×4 and ×5 tables (4, 8, 12… and 5, 10, 15, 20… circled) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 77 |
| Consolidation: complete the numbers on the circles (6, 9, □, 15, □, □, 24, □): by how much is each number divisible, can all the numbers be divided by the same number, sort out the odd numbers and double them by addition, write the numbers divisible by 2 separately, and which is a number whose tens digit is 2 times smaller than its ones digit?; bundle the balloons in threes and put one bundle in one row — how many rows are there? then bundle them in sixes and in nines and repeat the action; write the operation sign that makes each equality true (10 □ 5 = 15; 5 □ 6 = 30; 40 □ 5 = 35; 42 □ 7 = 6; 3 □ 3 = 9; 90 □ 10 = 9; 27 □ 9 = 3; 81 □ 11 = 70); write expressions from words and evaluate (increase four sixfold then decrease by 10; decrease the product of 3 and 9 by 5; increase the product of 36 and 4?? as printed "36-г 4 дахин ихэсгээд 100-аар ихэсгэх"; increase the quotient of 81 and 9 by 9); the boy memorised 8 riddles this month and will memorise 3 times more riddles next month, and the total of riddles and poems he memorised is 6 times less than … — how many riddles and poems did he memorise this month?; craft competition problems (three boys each scored 4 points with their crafts; six girls each made 2 cars…; the girls entered the competition with 6 times fewer crafts than the boys' total — how many paper crafts did the girls enter?); Bat's teacher said: "How old are you?" — the teacher answered "My age is a number between 29 and 40 that is divisible by 6" — how old is Bat's teacher? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 78 |
| Multiplying by seven, dividing by seven: biscuits on plates — a table builds 1 × 7 = 7, 2 × 7 = 14, 3 × 7 = 21, 4 × 7, 5 × 7, 6 × 7, □ × 7 with +1 plate and +7 biscuit steps (seven plates pictured); compose an expression from the plum picture and solve it; draw a 70 mm segment and colour every 10 mm in a different colour — how many colours did you use?; Sugar found the ×7 products quickly using the ×5 and ×2 tables: complete the unfinished writings (2 × 2 = 4 and 2 × 5 = 10 give 2 × 7 = 4 + 10 = 14; 3 × 2 = 6 and 3 × 5 = 15 give 3 × 7; 6 × 2 and 6 × 5 give □ × □ = □ + □); using the ×5 and ×2 tables' products, say the ×7 products; can the ×6 products be found from the ×5 and ×7 tables — test it | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 80 |
| Tables consolidation with time — consolidation: evaluate (40 − 24 ÷ 8 + 19; 56 ÷ 14?? as printed "56 + 14 − 4 × 8"; 6 × 7 + 8 × 8; 7 × 9 − 54 ÷ 6); draw a table with 4 rows and 14 columns, divide it into 7 equal parts and colour each part differently; compose problems matching the templates (4 × □ = 36 with □ × 4 = 36; 42 ÷ 7 = □ with 42 ÷ □ = 7); a shop-hours problem: on Saturday Sargalan came to the shop with her mother at 3 o'clock and the shop was closed — why was it closed (the sign: Mon-Fri 09:00-14:00, Sat 10:00-14:00, Sun closed)? if they had come how many hours earlier could they have been served? how many hours did the shop work today? if today had been Thursday and they had come at closing time, how many hours earlier should they have come? how many hours in all does the shop work in 7 days?; brick pyramids (30 resting on 15, 15, 15; 80 over 40, with 20 and 10 below); compose examples matching shape templates (○ × △ ÷ △ = ○; ■ ÷ ■ × △ = △; ○ × ○ × △ = △ as printed) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 82 |
| Doubling a two-digit number — increasing a two-digit number twofold (2 оронтой тоог 2 дахин ихэсгэх): a. observe the products and complete them (2 × 1 = 2; 2 × 3 = 6; 2 × 4 = 8; 2 × 2 = □; 2 × 5 = □; 2 × 6 = □; 2 × 10 = 20; 2 × 30 = 60; 2 × 40 = 80; 2 × 20 = □; 2 × 50 = □; 2 × 60 = □): how do the equalities in the 1st row differ from those in the 2nd row — discuss; b. find the products by the method above (2 × 80; 2 × 90); c. discuss what an easy method is for multiplying a whole-tens number: the girl — when a number is multiplied by whole tens, the product ends in zero; the boy — write the zero of the tens after the number (example: 60 × 2 = 120 because 6 × 2 = 12); a game "Double the number": write the numbers of the symmetric shapes in your notebook; choose one of the shapes whose number you wrote, draw and cut it out; check whether it is symmetric; fold the cut shape, mark 12 dots with strokes on one side of the fold line of the unfolded shape, and fold it; how did the first shape's dot count change when folded — write the operation; there are 2 sacks of apples of the same weight, each sack has 23 kg of apples — how many kilograms of apples are in the 2 sacks, and how is it found? Khulan finds the total weight of the 2 sacks of apples by the method below: 2 × 23 splits into 2 × 20 = 40 and 2 × 3 = 6, totalling 46; is there another, easier method than the one above — discuss; Dolgion and Dölgöön bought the same number of each fruit from 2 shops: using Khulan's method, find the numbers of fruit they bought (Dolgion's row: bananas 12, apples 30, pears 24, oranges 25 — doubled for the two buyers) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 122 |
| Doubling strategies — complete the matching numbers (two brick pyramids built on 20, 20, 20, 20 with 40 above, and on 12, 24, 48); do it on a calculator machine with 20 × 2: the equals sign each time gives the next number — compare the number of digits of each resulting number with the number of shapes; how did the 2 actions above differ — explain; the boy: 2 × 22 = 44; 2 × 4 = 8 is known — using this knowledge, how is 2 × 26 found?; find the products in each column by the method above (2 × 31, 2 × 37; 2 × 44, 2 × 48; 2 × 45, 2 × 49; 2 × 54, 2 × 56); Saruul read 46 words in a minute and Telmen read 2 times more words than Saruul — how many words did Telmen read in a minute?; one tent (майхан) takes 27 metres of fabric to sew: the elder sister sewed 1 tent and the younger sister sewed 2 times more tents than the elder — how many metres of fabric did each of them use?; compose expressions matching the templates — the same letter expresses the same number (a. A + A = B; b. A × C = B; c. A × A = A) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 123 |
| Consolidation: building tables from other tables — find the total length of the road Mönkh travelled (a route picture: 250 m by horse, 400 m by car, 140 m on foot, 110 m by bicycle); Dorj's 17 sheep lambed: 4 of them had twins — how many lambs does Dorj have in all?; using the number cards 6, 1, 5, 0, calculate: a. compose the biggest and smallest two-digit numbers and the biggest three-digit number and find their differences; b. compose the biggest two-digit number with all even digits and find the sum of its digits; c. find the biggest three-digit number and the smallest two-digit number and find their sum; d. write the two-digit numbers greater than 60; e. write the two-digit numbers less than 60 whose digits are all odd; on a divided line, for each of the numbers shown (435, 455, 515), find and mark the number 40 greater and 40 smaller than it; using the multiplication table's table, calculate: which multiplication tables can be built from the ×2 and ×4 tables? Build the ×7 table using the ×4 and ×3 tables; build other tables too — which table was the easiest to build, and why? Using the table of numbers 1-10, can you output the multiplication tables of numbers up to 20? Choose any 3 numbers and compose them | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 138 |
| Revision: arrays, calendar wheel, patterns — find the total number of mushrooms in the picture — by how many methods can it be calculated? Discuss (four arrays of mushrooms); classes 3a and 3b did cleaning: each class has 25 pupils — how many pupils did cleaning in all?; the class's 36 pupils lined up in rows of 9 — how many rows did they line up in?; observe the time wheel (a circular year calendar with months and day counts) and discuss what the numbers express; compare (1 dm 3 cm □ 1 dm 8 cm; 5 dm □ 5 cm; 1 dm 6 cm □ 17 cm); 9 children stood in each row: how many children stood in 10 rows — calculate by various methods; a sewing worker sewed 5 adults' deels (traditional coats) and 3 children's deels: 4 m of fabric goes into an adult's deel and 2 m into a child's deel — how many metres of fabric did the sewing worker use in all?; compare and calculate (35 ÷ 7 × 5; 15 ÷ 5 + 21; 76 − 9 × 3); name the shapes (a quadrilateral, a parallelogram, a triangle, a quadrilateral, a right triangle, a pentagon, a hexagon): how are the shapes alike and how are they different?; find the pattern and write the next three numbers (2, 4, 6, 8, …; 100, 90, 80, …; 3, 6, 9, …; 800, 700, 600, …; 75, 70, 65, …; 998, 992, 986, …); self-check: compare and calculate (49 + 3 × 7; 4 × 9 − 19); share 45 flowers equally into 9 parts so that each bundle has the same number of flowers — how many flowers go into one bundle? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 5 |
| Multiplication laws from a tree array — multiplying and dividing within ten thousand: worked example 1: the numbers of trees planted around the school are shown in a picture (an array of 8 columns by 7 rows of triangles: green triangles are birches, red triangles are poplars; the first 3 columns are birches (21) and the other 5 columns are poplars (35)): each pupil calculated the total number of trees: calculation 1: 21 + 35 = 56 (total trees); calculation 2: 35 + 21 = 56 — with the box: 21 + 35 = 35 + 21 and "when the addends' places are swapped, the sum's value does not change"; calculation 3: 8 × 7 = 56; calculation 4: 7 × 8 = 56 — with the box: 8 × 7 = 7 × 8 (squares and a circle as symbols) and "when the factors' places are swapped, the product's value does not change"; calculation 5: (3 + 5) × 7 = 56 — a boy explains: "When multiplying a sum by a number, multiply each addend by that number and add the values that come out"; calculation 6: 3 × 7 + 5 × 7 = 56 — with the symbol box (■ + ▲) × ● = ■ × ● + ▲ × ● and (3 + 5) × 7 = 7 × (3 + 5); observe the picture in worked example 1 and find by how many the poplars are more than the birches: calculation 1: (5 − 3) × 7 = 14 (the difference); calculation 2: 5 × 7 − 3 × 7 = 14 — a girl explains: "When multiplying a difference by a number, multiply the number being subtracted from and the number being subtracted by that number and subtract the values that come out"; the symbol box: (■ − ▲) × ● = ■ × ● − ▲ × ● and (5 − 3) × 7 = 7 × (5 − 3) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 51 |
| Building the 11-times table; associative law — using the rule for multiplying a sum by a number, compose a multiplication table for numbers like 12, 13, 14, 15 by one of them — by 11 (Э.хүлээвэл — for example): the worked list: 1 × 11 = 1 × (10 + 1) = 1 × 10 + 1 × 1 = 10 + 1 = 11; 2 × 11 = 2 × (10 + 1) = 2 × 10 + 2 × 1 = 20 + 2 = 22; 3 × 11 = 33; 4 × 11 = 44; 5 × 11 = 55; 6 × 11 = 66; 7 × 11 = 77; 8 × 11 = 88; 9 × 11 = 99; 10 × 11 = 10 × (10 + 1) = 10 × 10 + 10 × 1 = 100 + 10 = 110; the 11-times table is listed (1 × 11 = 11 up to 10 × 11 = 110); a girl asks: "Using this method, a multiplication table for which number can be composed?"; in the library, the first shelf has 342 books, the second shelf 263 books and the third shelf 237 books: how many books are there on the three shelves? By how many methods can it be calculated (the symbol box: ■ + ▲ + ● = (■ + ▲) + ● = ■ + (▲ + ●) — "it can also be calculated by grouping"); the class's 11 pupils went on a trip: in each box there were 4 bottles of juice drink: if each pupil took one box… — how many bottles of juice drink did they take in all? Calculate using the template (the symbol box: ■ × ▲ × ● = (■ × ▲) × ● = ■ × (▲ × ●)); discuss which property of the operations makes the calculation easy (a. 17 + 752 and 38 + 162; b. 5 × 16 and 8 × 17; c. (452 + 29) + 171 and (15 × 5) × 2); observe the pattern and continue it (triangles built from small triangles: the 1st picture has 1 small triangle = 1 × 1; the 2nd has 4 = 2 × 2; the 3rd has 9 = 3 × 3; the next boxes are empty) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 52 |
| Multiplying and dividing by 10, 100, 1000 — the abbreviations box: 10 ones = 1 ten (10 н = 1 а); 10 tens = 1 hundred (10 а = 1 з); 10 hundreds = 1 thousand (10 з = 1 мян); 10 thousands = 1 ten-thousand (10 мян = 1 а.мян); and back: 1 ten = 10 ones; 1 hundred = 10 tens = 100 ones; 1 thousand = 10 hundreds = 100 tens = 1000 ones; observe the operations done using the place table and explain them: a. multiplication shown with arrows in the place table: 10 × 1 = 10 ones = 1 ten = 10; 10 × 100 = 10 hundreds = 1 thousand = 1000; 10 × 10 × 10 = 100 × 10 = 1000; b. division: 1000 ÷ 10 = 10 hundreds ÷ 10 = 1 hundred = 100; 1000 ÷ 100 = 10 tens ÷ 10 tens = 10 (as printed); 1000 ÷ 10 ÷ 10 = 1000 ÷ (10 × 10) = 10; a boy's speech: "When a number is multiplied by 10 or 100, every digit of the number increases by 1 place in value. And when divided by 10 or 100, every digit of the number decreases by 1 place in value"; worked example 2: explain the calculations: a. when buying 5 sheets of ten-tögrög paper, how many tögrög are paid? 5 × 10 = 5 × 1 ten = 5 tens = 50 (tögrög); b. when buying 4 pens at a hundred tögrög each from a hundred-tögrög note (?) — 4 × 100 = 4 × 1 hundred = 4 hundreds = 400 (tögrög); worked example 3: observe the calculations and explain them: a. in each envelope there were ten hundred-tögrög notes: how many tögrög are there in five envelopes? 5 × 10 × 100 = 5 × 1000 = 5000 (tögrög); b. 8000 tögrög was split into hundreds and given to 10 children: how many hundred-notes did each child receive? 8000 ÷ 100 ÷ 10 = 8000 ÷ (100 × 10) = 8000 ÷ 1000 = 8 (hundred-notes); a girl's speech: "Multiplying by 10 and also by 100 is the same as multiplying by 1000. Dividing by 10 and also by 100 is the same as dividing by 1000" | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 53 |
| Multiplying round numbers — find the products' values (2 × 10; 20 × 10; 20 × 100; 2 × 1000; 60 × 10; 600 × 10; 600 × 100; 6 × 1000; 10 × 120; 100 × 12; 1000 × 9; 10 × 730; 100 × 59; 10 × 310; 100 × 75; 1000 × 4); find the quotients' values (20 ÷ 10; 200 ÷ 10; 200 ÷ 100; 2000 ÷ 1000; 60 ÷ 10; 600 ÷ 10; 600 ÷ 100; 6000 ÷ 1000; 2400 ÷ 10; 2400 ÷ 100; 9000 ÷ 1000; 5900 ÷ 10; 5900 ÷ 100; 10000 ÷ 10; 10000 ÷ 100; 10000 ÷ 1000); remembering the multiplication table, complete the table (a factor grid: the products 56, 42, 72, 18, 45, 36, 24, 49, 81, 35, 54 are given and the missing factors are to be filled); complete the calculations and explain them (pictures: paper 10₮, a notebook 30₮, a pen 300₮, a book 3000₮): a. when buying 2 sheets of ten-tögrög paper, how much is paid? b. when buying 2 notebooks of plain paper at 30 tögrög, how much is paid (2 × 30 = 2 × 3 tens = … tens = …)? c. when buying 2 pens at 300 tögrög, how much (2 × 300 = 2 × 3 hundreds = …)? d. when buying 2 books at 3000 tögrög, how much (2 × 3000 = 2 × 3 thousands = …)?; worked example 4: observe the calculations' differences and explain them (3 × 20 = 3 × 2 tens = 6 tens = 60; 3 × 200 = 3 × 2 hundreds = 6 hundreds = 600; 3 × 2000 = 3 × 2 thousands = 6 thousands = 6000; and the other way: 3 × 20 = 3 × 2 × 10 = 6 × 10 = 60; 3 × 200 = 3 × 2 × 100 = 6 × 100 = 600; 3 × 2000 = 3 × 2 × 1000 = 6 × 1000 = 6000); find the products' values (5 × 10; 100 × 5; 1000 × 5; 9 × 7; 90 × 7; 900 × 7; 700 × 9; 30 × 3; 300 × 3; 3000 × 3; 7 × 60; 600 × 7) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 54 |
| Column multiplication: partial products, then compact — worked example 6: observe and explain (bar models: 3 × 12 with the bar split into 10 and 2: 3 × 12 breaks into 3 × 10 and 3 × 2: 3 × 10 + 3 × 2 = 30 + 6 = 36; 3 × 12 = 3 × (10 + 2) = 3 × 10 + 3 × 2 = 30 + 6 = 36; 3 × 312 with the bar split into 300, 10 and 2: 3 × 312 = 3 × (300 + 10 + 2) = …); the vertical writings with partial products: 12 × 3 (3 × 2 = 6; 3 × 10 = 30; total 36) and 312 × 3 (3 × 2 = 6; 3 × 10 = 30; 3 × 300 = 900; total 936); calculate (3 × 13; 3 × 130; 3 × 213; 4 × 21; 4 × 210; 4 × 321; 32 × 2; 320 × 2; 332 × 2; 233 × 3; 434 × 2; 313 × 3; 123 × 2; 1230 × 2; 230 × 3; 1132 × 3; 1221 × 4); worked example 7: observe and explain (79 × 6 in columns with partial products: 6 × 9 = 54; 6 × 70 = 420; total 474; 479 × 6: 6 × 9 = 54; 6 × 70 = 420; 6 × 400 = 2400; total 2874); explain how the value of the product 6 × 479 was found in the compact column (three stages shown: first the ones: 6 × 9 = 54 — write 4, carry 5 over the tens digit; then the tens: 6 × 7 = 42, 42 + 5 = 47 — write 7, carry 4; then the hundreds: 6 × 4 = 24, 24 + 4 = 28 — giving 2874) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 56 |
| Multiplying via ×10 splitting; money problems — think about the method of calculation and do the operations (the example: 2 × 130 = 2 × 13 × 10 = 26 × 10 = 260; then: 14 × 5; 140 × 5; 16 × 5; 160 × 6; 34 × 4; 340 × 4; 3 × 85; 3 × 850; 9 × 27; 3 × 270; 25 × 8; 250 × 8); do the operations (9 × 74; 528 × 4; 4 × 69; 192 × 4 (as printed in the set); 8 × 75; 317 × 6; 46 × 6; 6 × 890; 7 × 1038; 1447 × 4; 2 × 3690; 3 × 1978); worked example 8: explain the calculation (209 × 6 in columns: the partial products 6 × 9 = 54 and 6 × 200 = 1200 give 1254; then the compact stages: 6 × 9 = 54 — write 4, carry 5; the tens: 6 × 0 + 5 = 5; the hundreds: 6 × 2 = 12 — giving 1254); calculate (9 × 309; 2 × 506; 408 × 7; 604 × 5; 6 × 1207; 3 × 2902); a game "Multiply the number by tens": prepare two packs of cards with numbers from 1 to 9 and from 10 to 90: a. become a group of three pupils: a chooser, a guesser and a doer; b. the chooser chooses 1 card from each of the two packs; c. the performer multiplies the two chosen numbers together; d. the guesser, having calculated mentally, says the answer, and it is checked on the spot; e. the players swap roles and play; from a roll of ribbon 32 cm long, Bat took 4 bundles (боодол) and Tuya took 6 bundles: find the total length of the ribbon each child took; twin girls like to collect the same things: one of them collected 26 badges, 49 pictures, 15 letters and 28 books: if the other one collected the same numbers of things, how many badges, pictures, letters and books did they collect in all?; 6 pupils each contributed 250 tögrög and bought a juice drink together: what was the price of the juice drink they bought (6 × 250)?; a number was decreased 4 times and increased by 396, and 544 came out: find that number ((544 − 396) × 4); the product value of which four-digit and one-digit numbers is bigger than 6000 and smaller than 6200? How many different variants did you find? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 57 |
| Multiplying by 9 and by 11; composing problems — is there an easy method to find the sum's value of all the even numbers smaller than 27?; compose problems with the numbers 379, 6 and 259 and solve them: swap your composed problem with your classmate and solve it: what other problem can be composed?; worked example 9: explain the calculations (28 × 9 = 28 × (10 − 1) = 28 × 10 − 28 × 1 = 280 − 28 = 252; 28 × 11 = 28 × (10 + 1) = 28 × 10 + 28 × 1 = 280 + 28 = 308; 317 × 9 = 317 × (10 − 1) = 317 × 10 − 317 × 1 = 3170 − 317 = 2853; 317 × 11 = 317 × (10 + 1) = 317 × 10 + 317 × 1 = 3170 + 317 = 3487); calculate (2 × 90; 28 × 9; 28 × 11; 12 × 100; 128 × 9; 128 × 11; 350 × 10; 65 × 11; 7 × 1000; 94 × 11; 715 × 10; 327 × 11; 3 × 9000; 493 × 9; 509 × 11); calculate (900 × 2 + 450; 8 × 120 − 519; 597 × 2 − 210; 699 × 3 + 3560; 808 × 9 + 159 × 11; 818 × 12 − 18 × 12); Bayaraa bought 4 notebooks at 600 tögrög, 2 pens at 750 tögrög and 1 drawing notebook at 1250 tögrög: the seller wrote it as the expression 4 × 600 + 2 × 750 + 1250: find the total value: how is each expression read?; find the products' values mentally (4 × 100; 45 × 10; 270 × 10; 18 × 100; 672 × 10; 410 × 10; 200 × 40; 30 × 200; 28 × 300; 72 × 100; 340 × 10; 50 × 160); Bold can buy identical notebooks with 1000 tögrög: in the shop there are notebooks at 240₮, 300₮ and 400₮: which notebook can Bold buy, and how many of them?; a self-check activity "Compose a number and multiply": each pupil prepares cards with digits 0-9: a. from the digits, compose one-digit, two-digit and three-digit numbers; b. tell your friend your numbers; c. multiply your friend's two-digit and three-digit numbers by a one-digit number; d. swap notebooks and check whether the multiplication was done correctly | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 58 |
| Consolidation (Бататгах): calculate and draw a conclusion: a. multiply the numbers 43, 69, 84 by 10, then multiply the resulting numbers by 10 again; multiply each given number by 100; b. divide the numbers 2500, 8700, 1900 by 10, then divide the resulting numbers by 10 again; divide each given number by 100; a boy collected money to buy a gift for his mother and father: complete the calculation by counting the collected money (the table: 126 notes of 10₮; 15 notes of 100₮; 3 notes of 1000₮: 126 × 1 ten = 126 tens; 126 × 10 = □ ₮; 15 × 1 hundred = 15 hundreds; 15 × 100 = □ ₮; 3 × 1 thousand = 3 thousands; 3 × 1000 = □ ₮): how many tögrög did the boy collect in all (1260 + 1500 + 3000 = 5760)?; calculate: a. 100 tens, 40 hundreds and 5 thousand-notes together make how much? b. 250 tens, 35 hundreds and 4 thousand-notes together make how much?; calculate (287 + 598; 250 × 5 × 4; 375 − 299; 35 × 7 × 2; 899 − 270; 320 × 5 ÷ 4; 3400 + 276 + 1600; 1580 × 4 ÷ 5; 307 × 9); observe the example and calculate (375 × 5 = 375 × 10 ÷ 2 = 3750 ÷ 2 = 1875; then 683 × 5; 932 × 5; 903 × 5 — multiplying by 5 via multiplying by 10 and halving); the children planted 56 sea-buckthorn bushes in 4 days: on the first day they planted 14 bushes and on the second day 2 times more bushes than on the first day: could the children have planted equal numbers of bushes on the third and fourth days? Why? (56 − 14 − 28 = 14, and 14 = 7 + 7, so yes); 140 apples were bagged 3 at a time: how many bags were made? How many apples were left unbagged (140 ÷ 3 = 46 remainder 2)?; do the multiplications and explain them (7 × 8 = 56 in a digit grid; 7 × 10 = 70; 7 × 18 = 126 — built as 56 + 70; 8 × 9 and 8 × 20 and 8 × 29 in grids; 332 × 20 = 6640; 332 × 4 = 1328; 332 × 24 = 6640 + 1328); calculate (23 × 8; 62 × 7; 32 × 90; 41 × 50; 67 × 24; 28 × 54; 70 × 54; 36 × 43; 45 × 38; 145 × 30; 145 × 2; 145 × 32; 326 × 20; 326 × 8; 326 × 28) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 62 |
| Consolidation: multiplying/dividing within 10,000 — multiplying and dividing numbers within ten thousand: find the products' and quotients' values (223 × 4; 728 ÷ 4; 274 × 3; 528 ÷ 4; 312 × 2; 360 ÷ 2; 145 × 6; 280 ÷ 40; 642 × 6; 720 ÷ 20; 460 × 6; 700 ÷ 10); complete the actions and discuss the results (four flow diagrams carrying numbers through +100, ×100, +100 and −100 arrows: 5, 50, 500, 9900; 4, 40, 59, 100; 100, 900, 6700, 10000); Khaliun planned to buy toy pictures with the money she had collected: a toy picture costs 100₮ each: if she has 3160₮, what is the most toy pictures she can buy (31)?; a vegetable grower packed 363 kg of cucumbers into boxes of 5 kg capacity to store them in a cellar: how many boxes are needed (73)?; some round-trip travellers planned to travel for 2 days to their final point: because of the weather, on the first day they travelled 40 km less than planned: to arrive in their time, how many kilometres more must they travel on the 2nd day (40 km more)?; compose problems using the goods' quantities and measures given in the table and solve them (notebooks: 3 pieces cost 4500₮, so 3 × 2 pieces cost □; coloured pencils: 8 cost 6400₮, so 8 ÷ 4 cost □; pens: 5 cost 3250₮, so 5 × 2 cost □ — the same total scaled with the quantity); in the shop's warehouse, boxes are stacked in piles: in each box there are 6 glass cups: find the number of glass cups in these boxes (a three-dimensional stack of boxes is pictured: count the boxes and multiply by 6); complete the sentences (a. if one addend of a sum is increased by 100 and the other is decreased by 50, the sum's value …… by 50; b. if the dividend is increased 2 times, the quotient's value increases …… times; c. if the minuend is increased by 50 and the subtrahend is increased by 50, the difference's value does not change); find the quotients' values and explain using the pattern (2400 ÷ 100 and 4800 ÷ 200, where the dividend and divisor both double; 360 ÷ 20 and 3600 ÷ 2; 9000 ÷ 1000 and 4500 ÷ 500, where both halve); Bayaraa's family uses 120 litres of water in a day: how many litres of water will they use in 10 days? In a month? If 1 litre of water costs 2₮, how many tögrög will they spend on water use in a month?; compare the calculations (5039 − 916 □ 6000 − 916; 2603 + 376 □ 376 + 2603; (28 + 15) × 4 □ 28 × 4 + 15 × 4; 685 × 10 □ 683 × 10); one organisation received 775 presents: 55 of them were given to a kindergarten and the rest were divided equally among 9 schools: how many presents did each school receive (80)?; solve the problems: what difference is there in their calculations (a. 47 × 9; b. 9 pupils from each of 47 schools took part in a competition: how many pupils took part in all?; c. potatoes were packed into 47 bags of 9 kg each: how many kilograms of potatoes were packed in all? — all three are 47 × 9 in different settings); a self-check: 1) a mine's extraction section has 1200 workers and its processing section 1500 workers: as the work grew, the extraction section added 500 workers and the processing section added 400: how did the mine's total number of workers change (it grew by 900)?; 2) insert the fitting numbers in the boxes (how a product stays 240 when one factor is divided and the other is multiplied); 3) there is a 17-litre container of water and a 5-litre vessel: how can exactly 7 litres of water be taken (fill the 17 l, pour off 5 l twice: 7 l remain)? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed pp. 132–133 |
| Multiplying by a one- and two-digit number (Нэг ба хоёр оронтой тоогоор үржүүлэх): if a boy buys four notebooks at 1500₮ (five, ten), how many tögrög does he pay? Discuss how you found it by the easy method (the picture splits the price into five 1000₮ notes and five 500₮ notes: 5 × 1000 = 5000 and 5 × 500 = 2500; also by repeated addition; 1500 × 2 = 3000, 1500 × 4 = 6000, 1500 × 10 = 15000, 1500 × 5 = 7500, with the doubling and halving arrows; the table of × (1000, 500) for the factor 5); discuss the multiplication methods and choose the easy method for yourself (the three methods for 78 × 2 = 156 shown side by side: Method I, the place grid — 70 × 2 = 140 and 8 × 2 = 16, then 140 + 16; Method II, the diagonal lattice grid; Method III, the written expansion — 78 = 7 tens + 8 ones: (70 + 8) × 2 = 70 × 2 + 8 × 2, and also (80 − 2) × 2 = 80 × 2 − 2 × 2; a girl's rule: "Write the number as a sum or a difference of place addends, multiply each term, and find the sum of the products"; a boy's rule: "If the number is written as a sum or a difference of suitable numbers, each term is multiplied by the number and the sum of the products is the product's value"); choose a suitable method and find the products' values (265 × 2; 265 × 4; 265 × 8; 265 × 10; 265 × 5); observe and complete the writing 765 × 3 = ?, and say the value mentally (three forms side by side: the area model with 700, 60, 5 giving 2100, 180, 15; the diagonal lattice for 765 × 3 = 2295; and the written forms 785 × 2 = (700 + 85) × 2 = … and 785 × 2 = (800 − 15) × 2 = …); calculate (365 × 10 + 365 × 2 = ?; 365 × 20 + 365 × 5 = □; 365 × 21 = ?); a self-check: explain what number must be multiplied by what, and how, to find the unknown term ((□ + 25) × 2 = 800 + ○; △ × (300 + 18) = 600 + □); check the children's calculations and explain what the mistake is (the boy's and the girl's workings of 121 × 100, 359 × 100 and 3590 × 100, with their wrong counts of noughts — 121 000, 359 00, 121 00, 359 000, 359 00 — a find-the-error exercise on appending zeros); observe the calculation and find the product by the easy method (a. 170 × 9 = 170 × (10 − 1) = 1700 − 170 = 1530; b. 253 × 11 = 253 × (10 + 1) = 2530 + 253 — see the note: the book prints the final result as 1683, which is wrong; 2530 + 253 = 2783); then the practice set (2530 × 9; 1263 × 11; 7950 × 5; 4006 × 99; 4251 × 25; 1572 × 50); choose a method and multiply (four methods shown in full for 2498 × 9 = 22482: Method 1, the full place expansion (2000 + 400 + 90 + 8) × 9 = 18000 + 3600 + 810 + 72; Method 2, ×(10 − 1): 24980 − 2498; Method 3, (2500 − 2) × 9 = 22500 − 18; Method 4, the column writing with the partial products 72, 810, 3600, 18000, and the "easy writing" — the compact column with the carries 4, 8, 7 written above); find the product following the pattern (△ + ●) × ■ = △ × ■ + ● × ■ (the table: the sums and differences 300 + 6, 260 + 4, 500 − 8, 150 − 5 against the factors 2, 3, 4, 5); find the rectangles' frame lengths (the table: the lengths 245, 1478, 530, 125, 209 cm against the widths 85, 96, 75, 84, 68 cm — the perimeter row to fill); a self-check activity: multiply the three-digit number your friend says by 8: let each pupil race to calculate the numbered examples, watching the time | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 32–33 |
| Two-digit multipliers: column method and patterns — the cabbage and potato fields have equal lengths and widths of 5 m and 10 m: find the measure of each vegetable's field and the measure of the whole field (the diagram: the total length 198 m; potatoes 198 × 10, cabbage 198 × 5, and 198 × (10 + 5) = ?; the column forms shown: 198 × 5 = 990 in compact writing, and 198 × 15 expanded — 198 × 5 ones = 990, 198 × 1 ten = 1980, total 2970); observe the two children's calculations and discuss them (the girl's: 198 × 5 = (100 + 90 + 8) × 5 = 500 + 450 + 40 = 990, then 198 × 15; the boy's: 198 × 5 = (200 − 2) × 5 = 1000 − 10 = 990, then 198 × 15 = (200 − 2) × 15 = ?); carry out the actions (the column multiplications pictured, with 2523 × 2 = 5046 worked as the pattern: 2523 × 42; 4237 × 28; 4230 × 28); find the pattern in the products and discuss the terms' dependence (the table: a = 1111 throughout, b runs 11 to 17, and a × b runs 12221, 13332, 14443, … — the repunit pattern); discuss how the products were found (the grid models: a table × (40, 7) with the factor 5 gives 200 and 35, total 235; × (300, 8) with the factor 4 gives 1200 and 32; × (500, 10, 2) with the factor 3 gives 1500, 30, 6; × (1000, 50, 7) with the factor 3); a self-check: using the factors' place-swapping property (commutativity), find the products (32 × 123; 21 × 3052; 48 × 427; 15 × 2501); a coin problem: Gantömör collected 150 ten-möngö coins, and his father's collection of ten-möngö coins relates to his by a factor of 19: how many ten-möngö coins did father and son collect, and how many tögrög does father and son's collected money make in all (the pictured 10-möngö coin, with the note: 1 tögrög = 100 möngö); find the products' values by the easy method (a. using the rounding to a round tens number: 15 × 19 = 15 × 20 − 15 × 1; 15 × 21 = 15 × 20 + 15 × 1; 21 × 15 = 20 × 15 + 1 × 15; b. how it is easy to find the products of two-digit numbers up to 20: 15 × 19 = ((5 × 9) + 100) + (5 + 9) × 10 = 145 + 140 = 285 — the boxes: "add 100 to the product of the ones" and "multiply the sum of the ones by 10"; a boy's note: "Learn the easy methods of finding a product's value mentally, and use them"); observe the writing and find the product by the easy method (38 × 19 worked as 760 − 38 = 722; 38 × 21 worked as 760 + 38; 47 × 19; 47 × 21); 428 × 11 = ?: how was it multiplied easily (the girl's column: 428 × 10 = 4280, plus 428 × 1 = 428, total 4708); calculate the products' values mentally by the above method and explain (35 × 20; 35 × 21; 458 × 10; 458 × 11; 254 × 20; 254 × 19; 750 × 20; 750 × 21); an activity: two pupils draw 2 kinds of number cards and say their products to each other mentally: if the product is said correctly the pupil scores a point (each card has a two-digit number and a one-digit number, written on its back) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 34–35 |
| Mental multiplication: the 9-finger trick and shortcut methods — let's learn to multiply mentally (Цээжээр үржүүлэх сурцгаая): let's look at the 9 times table on our fingers: spread both hands' fingers and, starting from the left hand's thumb, number the fingers from 1 to 10: the bent finger's left-hand side gives the tens-place number and its right-hand side gives the ones-place number (the photographs show 1 × 9 = 9 three ways: 0 tens and 9 ones); explain the methods of multiplying a number by 2, 5, 10, 25, 50, 100 (the table of methods: × 5 — multiply the number by 10 and divide by 2; × 9 — multiply the number by 10 and subtract the number itself; × 11 — multiply the number by 10 and add the number itself, or write the digit-sum between the digits of a two-digit number, as in 24 × 11 = 264 with 2 + 4 inserted, and 48 × 11 = 528 with 4 + 8 = 12 carried; × 50 — multiply the number by 100 and divide by 2, as in 14 × 50 = 1400 ÷ 2 = 700; × 25 — multiply the number by 100 and divide by 4 — see the note: the book's printed examples for this table contain errors: the × 5, × 9 and × 11 (first method) examples are labelled with the number 24 but worked with the number 14 (printed "24 × 5 = 140 ÷ 2 = 70", "24 × 9 = 140 − 14 = 126", "24 × 11 = 140 + 14 = 154" — for 24 the correct values are 120, 216 and 264), and the × 25 example prints "14 × 25 = 1400 ÷ 4 = 150", where 1400 ÷ 4 = 350, so the correct value is 350); multiply mentally and tell your friend (48 × 5; 34 × 9; 48 × 25; 36 × 5; 40 × 11; 86 × 50; 75 × 5; 72 × 11; 72 × 50; 19 × 9; 18 × 11; 18 × 50; 25 × 9; 36 × 25; 25 × 11) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 36 |
| Squaring numbers ending in 5; multiplying by 25 and 50 — let's calculate mentally (Цээжээр тооцоолж сурцгаая): let's learn the interesting method of multiplying a two-digit number ending in five by itself (35 × 35 = 1225: the working shows 5 × 5 = 25, and the tens digit 3 multiplied by the digit 1 greater than it, 3 × 4 = 12, with 25 written behind the product; a girl's rule: "Multiply the tens-place digit by the digit 1 greater than it, and join 25 behind the product"); find the products' values mentally by the interesting method (15 × 15; 25 × 25; 35 × 35; 45 × 45; 55 × 55; 65 × 65; 75 × 75; 85 × 85); do an experiment yourselves to find the product 15 × 25 by an easy method; get acquainted with the easy methods of finding a product's value (428 × 25 = ?: the boy's column: 428 × 25 = 2140 + 8560 = 10700; and the other way: 428 × 100 ÷ 4 = 10700, worked as the division 42800 ÷ 4 in the grid); write the expressions' values following the example and discuss (352 × 50 as 352 × 100 ÷ 2; 715 × 50 as 715 × 100 ÷ 2; 129 × 50 as 129 × 100 ÷ 2; 234 × 100 ÷ 2 as 234 × 50); complete the table with the suitable numbers in your notebook (the rectangles table: the lengths 245, 1478, 530, 125, 209 cm and the widths 85, 96, 75, 84, 68 cm, with the rows for the perimeter in cm and the area in cm.kv to fill); find the expressions' values (136 × 24; 136 × 48; 506 × 28; 9212 × 9 − (43024 + 129 × 38)); find the equal values and write them as equalities (328 × 25 against 164 × 50; 328 × 5 × 5 against 164 × 10 × 5; 328 × 100 ÷ 4 against 21 × 400; 84 × 100 against 42 × 200); a self-check: a computer carries out 9456 actions in one second: how many actions does it carry out in 1 minute | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 40 |
| Mental calculation: multiplying by 111 and number patterns — let's calculate mentally (Цээжээр тооцоолж сурцгаая): observe the two easy calculation methods and discuss them: 26 × 111 = ? (the boy's column: 26 × 111 = 26 + 260 + 2600 = 2886; the girl's shortcut: 26 × 111 = 2886, with her rule: "If the digit sum is less than 10, when a two-digit number is multiplied by 111, write the digit sum twice between the number's digits" — 2, (2 + 6), (2 + 6), 6); a. find the products' values by the above method and check them (72 × 111; 45 × 111; 24 × 111; 36 × 111; 62 × 111; 81 × 111); b. when the digit sum is greater than 10, what pattern is there in multiplying a two-digit number by 111: experiment yourselves and draw a conclusion; c. what pattern is there in multiplying a three-digit number by 111: experiment and discuss; check whether the products' values are correct, and if correct mark ✓: discuss the terms' dependence and, continuing the pattern, find the products' values (the mountain patterns: 6 × 7 = 42; 66 × 67 = 4422; 666 × 667 = 444222; then □ × □ = □; 77 × 78 = 6006; 777 × 78 = 60606; 7777 × 78 = 606606; then □ × □ = □); find the unknown term and check it (the paw-print unknowns: paw × 15 = 1170; 9360 ÷ paw = 4; 7 × paw = 1449; paw ÷ 6789 = 19; paw ÷ 45 = 45); find the expressions' values (30000 − 762 × 42 ÷ 6 + 35263; 762 × 28 + 423 − 846 ÷ 2 − 3198); write the expression and find its value (from 400707 subtract the number built from 30764 and 13, decreased 25 times); two workers, one having worked 9 days and the other 11 days, received 628500 tögrög of wages together: if both received equal wages each day, find each worker's wages (the daily wage is 628500 ÷ 20, giving 282825₮ and 345675₮); find the unknown term and check it (A + 4508 = 67073; B ÷ 4073 = 800; 17783 − C = 9897) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 45 |
